Q1. Tens digit \(4\), digit sum \(12\): which two-digit number satisfies both facts?
A. \(50\)
B. \(48\)
C. \(49\)
D. \(47\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
4+u=12\Rightarrow u=8
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 48.

\[
N=10\times 4+8=48
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 48 is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(48\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=8
\]

\[
N=48
\]

Fast result: \(48\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---
Q2. Between \(183\) and \(273\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 183 is 195.

\[
15\times 13=195
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 273.

\[
273=15\times 18+3
\]

\[
273-3=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-195}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{273}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{182}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-12=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_divisibility_range_count
---
Q3. For the number \(480\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(480)=(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=24
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 24.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_factor_count_basic
---
Q4. After prime factorising \(108\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?
A. \(294\)
B. \(308\)
C. \(266\)
D. \(280\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(108)=280
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 280.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(280\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{4}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(280\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
---
Q5. For two numbers \(165\) and \(105\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(1155\)
B. \(1271\)
C. \(1386\)
D. \(1039\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
165=3\times 5\times 11
\]

\[
105=3\times 5\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,105)=1155
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1155.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1155\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,105)=\frac{165\times 105}{\operatorname{HCF}(165,105)}=1155
\]

Fast result: \(1155\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_hidden_hcf

Q7. Power \(3^{47}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(47-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
3^{47}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(47-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
3^{47}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_modular_cycle
---

Q9. Number \(81\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(65\)
B. \(63\)
C. \(64\)
D. \(62\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+1=81
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+8=18
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-1|=9\times 7=63
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 63.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(63\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-1|=63
\]

Fast result: \(63\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_digit_interchange
---
Q10. What is the highest power of \(5\) that divides \(75!\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
15+3=18
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 5 dividing 75! is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_highest_power_dividing

Q12. By what least number should \(60\) be multiplied so that the product is divisible by \(120\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
g=\gcd(60,120)=60
\]

\[
x=\frac{120}{g}=2
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 2.
\[
N=2
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_minimum_multiplier

Q14. What is the least perfect square that is divisible by \(200\)?
A. \(380\)
B. \(400\)
C. \(420\)
D. \(440\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
200=2^{3}\times 5^{2}
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{4}
\]

\[
5^{2}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
200\times 2=400
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=400
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(400\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=2
\]

Fast result: \(400\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_least_square_multiple

N=720

This makes the final candidate satisfy the full chain, not only the first clue.

\[
A=720
\]

Answer Verification
The reconstructed value is 720.
\[
N=720
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(720\).

Shortcut

\[
Z=720
\]

Fast result: \(720\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q19. Between \(170\) and \(339\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 170 is 180.

\[
18\times 10=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 339.

\[
339=18\times 18+15
\]

\[
339-15=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{324-180}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{339}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{169}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-9=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q21. For the number \(30\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(1012500\)
B. \(1215000\)
C. \(810000\)
D. \(891000\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 30 d=30^{d(30)/2}=30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 810000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors

\[

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(105,105)=105
\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]

Fast result: \(105\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q24. Power \(11^{69}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(69-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
11^{69}\equiv 8\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 9th term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(69-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

\[
11^{69}\equiv 8\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_nested_remainder

Q28. How many trailing zeroes are there in \(30!\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

Trailing zeroes come from factor pairs 2×5; count fives.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 30! = 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(36)=(2+1)\times (2+1)=9
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q38. Between \(239\) and \(396\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 239 is 240.

\[
15\times 16=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 390 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 396.

\[
396=15\times 26+6
\]

\[
396-6=390
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{390-240}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{396}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{238}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-15=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\]

Fast result: \(378\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q41. When \(11^{51}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(51-1)\bmod 12+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
11^{51}\equiv 5\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(51-1)\bmod 12+1=3
\]

\[
11^{51}\equiv 5\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 128 is 132.

\[
6\times 22=132
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 218.

\[
218=6\times 36+2
\]

\[
218-2=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{216-132}{6}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{218}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{127}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-21=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q59. After prime factorising \(24\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(364954\)
B. \(414720\)
C. \(497664\)
D. \(331776\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(24)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 24 d=24^{d(24)/2}=24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 331776.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(331776\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Fast result: \(331776\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,150)=1650
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1650.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1650\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,150)=\frac{165\times 150}{\operatorname{HCF}(165,150)}=1650
\]

Fast result: \(1650\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q62. When \(11^{75}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(75-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
11^{75}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(75-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
4^{75}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Fast result: \(56\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\frac{348-192}{12}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{349}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{186}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
29-15=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q86. When \(7^{55}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(55-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{55}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(55-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
7^{55}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[

\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
5^{1}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=5
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q115. The number \(5x781\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=21
\]

\[
S+x=21+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[

\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 20 d=20^{d(20)/2}=20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 8000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(8000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q119. Power \(3^{80}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(80-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{80}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(80-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
3^{80}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q124. Between \(194\) and \(333\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 194 is 200.

\[
8\times 25=200
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 328 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 333.

\[
333=8\times 41+5
\]

\[
333-5=328
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{328-200}{8}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{333}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{193}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
41-24=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=19
\]

\[
19-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
19-x=11
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
19-8=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(90-1)\bmod 5+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{90}\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(90-1)\bmod 5+1=5
\]

\[
3^{90}\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(13\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 138 is 138.

\[
6\times 23=138
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 221.

\[
221=6\times 36+5
\]

\[
221-5=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{216-138}{6}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{221}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{137}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-22=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q157. The number \(610x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 13.

\[
13+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q172. Between \(238\) and \(366\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 238 is 240.

\[
15\times 16=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 366.

\[
366=15\times 24+6
\]

\[
366-6=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{360-240}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{366}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{237}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-15=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q176. When \(7^{30}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(30-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{30}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(30-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
7^{30}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q195. In \(25!\), what is the total count of trailing zeroes?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 25! = 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[

D. \(11\)
Answer: D

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(107-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 11.

\[
7^{107}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(107-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{107}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\[

\]

\[
N=216

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(216\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

\[
350=8\times 43+6
\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 21.
Hence, there are 21 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(21\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q228. The number \(292x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q231. When \(13^{29}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(29-1)\bmod 10+1=9
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
13^{29}\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 9th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(29-1)\bmod 10+1=9
\]

\[
2^{29}\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\[

Q254. Between \(205\) and \(288\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 205 is 208.

\[
8\times 26=208
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 288.

\[
288=8\times 36+0
\]

\[
288-0=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{288-208}{8}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{288}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{204}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-25=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q255. For exponent \(56\), what ending digit does \(8^{56}\) have?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
8\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(56-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
8^{56}\equiv 6\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(56-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
8^{56}\equiv 6\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Step 1: Count powers of 2 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{2}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{4}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
20+10+5+2+1=38
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 40! is 38.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(38\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{16}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(38\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 180 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 188.

\[
188=9\times 20+8
\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{180-108}{9}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

\]

\[

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[
1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(81-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
11^{81}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(81-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
1^{81}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(35\)
B. \(38\)
C. \(36\)
D. \(37\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+3=73
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 3+7=37
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-3|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-3|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[

\]

\]

Fast result: \(78\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q303. When \(2^{49}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(49-1)\bmod 10+1=9
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
2^{49}\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 9th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(49-1)\bmod 10+1=9
\]

\[
2^{49}\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q315. Between \(227\) and \(333\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 227 is 234.

\[
9\times 26=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 333 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 333.

\[
333=9\times 37+0
\]

\[
333-0=333
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{333-234}{9}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{333}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{226}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
37-25=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(24,102)=408
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 408.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(408\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(408\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q317. When \(7^{96}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(96-1)\bmod 12+1=12
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{96}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 12th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(96-1)\bmod 12+1=12
\]

\[
7^{96}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q319. With tens \(5\) and ones \(2\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(27\)
B. \(28\)
C. \(26\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+2=52
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+5=25
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-2|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-2|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q337. Between \(176\) and \(267\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 176 is 180.

\[
18\times 10=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 267.

\[
267=18\times 14+15
\]

\[
267-15=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{252-180}{18}+1=4+1=5
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 5.
Hence, there are 5 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{267}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{175}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
14-9=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q339. Power \(7^{52}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(52-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{52}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(52-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
2^{52}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

B. \(9\)

D. \(8\)
Answer: B

\[
N_1=10\times 4+5=45
\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-5|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-5|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q359. The number \(898x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 31.

\[
31+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
31+x=36
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
31+5=36
\]
\[
36\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(75-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
7^{75}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(75-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{75}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q365. Between \(106\) and \(265\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 106 is 108.

\[
9\times 12=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 261 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 265.

\[
265=9\times 29+4
\]

\[
265-4=261
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{261-108}{9}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{265}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{105}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
29-11=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q369. The number \(785x3\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=7
\]

\[
7-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
7-x=0
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
7-7=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(658\)
Answer: A

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(99,63)=693
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 693.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(693\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(99,63)=\frac{99\times 63}{\operatorname{HCF}(99,63)}=693
\]

Fast result: \(693\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q375. Tens digit \(3\) and ones digit \(4\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(8\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+4=34
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+3=43
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-4|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-4|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

N=1728

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(77-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
2^{77}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(77-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
2^{77}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q394. Between \(187\) and \(334\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 187 is 198.

\[
18\times 11=198
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 334.

\[
334=18\times 18+10
\]

\[
334-10=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{324-198}{18}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{334}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{186}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-10=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer

\]

Fast result: \(1638\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q412. The number \(6x438\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 21; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q422. The number \(370x2\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 12; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
12+x=18
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
12+6=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(66,60)=660
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 660.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(660\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(66,60)=\frac{66\times 60}{\operatorname{HCF}(66,60)}=660
\]

Fast result: \(660\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification

\[
16-5=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5\).

Q435. Between \(205\) and \(321\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 205 is 207.

\[
9\times 23=207
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 321.

\[
321=9\times 35+6
\]

\[
321-6=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{315-207}{9}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{321}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{204}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
35-22=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(85-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
2^{85}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-1|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-1|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q445. Between \(122\) and \(238\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 122 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 234 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 238.

\[
238=18\times 13+4
\]

\[
238-4=234
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{234-126}{18}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{238}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{121}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
13-6=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

A. \(13\)
B. \(14\)

D. \(15\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(46-1)\bmod 8+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 13.

\[
19^{46}\equiv 13\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 13.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(46-1)\bmod 8+1=6
\]

\[
2^{46}\equiv 13\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q449. The number \(6x092\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q470. Between \(126\) and \(213\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 126 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 198 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 213.

\[
213=18\times 11+15
\]

\[
213-15=198
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{198-126}{18}+1=4+1=5
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 5.
Hence, there are 5 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{213}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{125}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
11-6=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q471. Power \(2^{92}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(92-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
2^{92}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(92-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
2^{92}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(3\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+8=88
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 8+8=88
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-8|=9\times 0=0
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-8|=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q495. How many trailing zeroes are there in \(75!\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

Trailing zeroes come from factor pairs 2×5; count fives.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
15+3=18
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 75! = 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{25}\right\rfloor

\]

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q519. The number \(22x07\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=7
\]

\[
7+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
7+x=11
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
7+4=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+3=43
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 3+4=34
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-3|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-3|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q530. Between \(190\) and \(300\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 190 is 198.

\[
9\times 22=198
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 297 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 300.

\[
300=9\times 33+3
\]

\[
300-3=297
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{297-198}{9}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{300}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{189}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
33-21=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 26.

\[
26+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
26+x=30
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
26+4=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q550. For exponent \(65\), the powers of \(17\) repeat in remainders modulo \(13\). What remainder appears?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
17\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(65-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
17^{65}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(65-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
4^{65}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q555. Between \(102\) and \(241\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 102 is 104.

\[
8\times 13=104
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 240 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 241.

\[
241=8\times 30+1
\]

\[
241-1=240
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{240-104}{8}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{241}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{101}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
30-12=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Fast result: \(224\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q566. The number \(88x61\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=-5
\]

\[
-5+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
-5+x=0
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
-5+5=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer

Q576. The number \(3x216\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 12; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
12+x=18
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
12+6=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q577. After prime factorising \(12\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?
A. \(29\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(30\)
D. \(28\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(12)=28
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 28.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{2}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q578. Power \(2^{81}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(81-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
2^{81}\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(81-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[
2^{81}\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q588. Between \(159\) and \(266\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 159 is 162.

\[
18\times 9=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 266.

\[
266=18\times 14+14
\]

\[
266-14=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{252-162}{18}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{266}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{158}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
14-8=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q599. The number \(270x2\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=11
\]

\[
S+x=11+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
11+x=18
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
11+7=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 234 is 234.

\[
9\times 26=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 369 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 369.

\[
369=9\times 41+0
\]

\[
369-0=369
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{369-234}{9}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{369}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{233}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
41-25=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q603. For exponent \(71\), the powers of \(17\) repeat in remainders modulo \(13\). What remainder appears?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
17\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(71-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
17^{71}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(71-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
4^{71}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q606. The number \(3x658\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 22; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q625. Bells ring at intervals of \(99\) and \(90\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(1089\)
B. \(940\)
C. \(990\)
D. \(1040\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
99=3^{2}\times 11
\]

\[
90=2\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(99,90)=990
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 990.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(990\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(99,90)=\frac{99\times 90}{\operatorname{HCF}(99,90)}=990
\]

Fast result: \(990\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q632. Between \(220\) and \(352\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(24\)
C. \(22\)
D. \(23\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 220 is 222.

\[
6\times 37=222
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 348 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 352.

\[
352=6\times 58+4
\]

\[
352-4=348
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{348-222}{6}+1=21+1=22
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 22.
Hence, there are 22 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(22\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{352}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{219}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
58-36=22
\]

Fast result: \(22\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q639. With tens \(5\) and ones \(9\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(36\)
B. \(37\)
C. \(35\)
D. \(38\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+9=59
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+5=95
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-9|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-9|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q656. The number \(7x216\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q657. Two cyclic events repeat every \(40\) and \(104\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(546\)
B. \(572\)
C. \(494\)
D. \(520\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
40=2^{3}\times 5
\]

\[
104=2^{3}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,104)=520
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 520.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(520\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,104)=\frac{40\times 104}{\operatorname{HCF}(40,104)}=520
\]

Fast result: \(520\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q658. Power \(13^{68}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(68-1)\bmod 10+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
13^{68}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(68-1)\bmod 10+1=8
\]

\[
2^{68}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division

Q666. Between \(179\) and \(359\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(30\)
B. \(31\)
C. \(29\)
D. \(32\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 179 is 180.

\[
6\times 30=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 354 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 359.

\[
359=6\times 59+5
\]

\[
359-5=354
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{354-180}{6}+1=29+1=30
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 30.
Hence, there are 30 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(30\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{359}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{178}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
59-29=30
\]

Fast result: \(30\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Final Answer

Q683. The number \(93x58\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=25
\]

\[
S+x=25+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
25+x=27
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
25+2=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]
\[

\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q699. Between \(192\) and \(353\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 192 is 195.

\[
15\times 13=195
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 345 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 353.

\[
353=15\times 23+8
\]

\[
353-8=345
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{345-195}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{353}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{191}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
23-12=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Q724. The number \(332x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 8.

\[
8+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
8+x=12
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
8+4=12
\]
\[
12\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).
