B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 21; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q735. When \(3^{37}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(37-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{37}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(37-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
3^{37}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_modular_cycle

D. \(25\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 2 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{2}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{4}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
15+7+3+1=26
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 30! is 26.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(26\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(26\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
416=12\times 34+8
\]

\[
12-8=4
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 4.
\[
N=4
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q751. The number \(1x438\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=16
\]

\[
S+x=16+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q752. Two cyclic events repeat every \(72\) and \(180\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(378\)
B. \(396\)
C. \(342\)
D. \(360\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
72=2^{3}\times 3^{2}
\]

\[
180=2^{2}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(72,180)=360
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 360.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(360\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(72,180)=\frac{72\times 180}{\operatorname{HCF}(72,180)}=360
\]

Fast result: \(360\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q757. Between \(240\) and \(408\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 240 is 243.

\[
9\times 27=243
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 405 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 408.

\[
408=9\times 45+3
\]

\[
408-3=405
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{405-243}{9}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{408}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{239}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
45-26=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

\[

Q771. The number \(432x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

2\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(28-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
2^{28}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(28-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

\[
2^{28}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_modular_cycle

Q792. The number \(236x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 11.

\[
11+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
11+x=15
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
11+4=15
\]
\[
15\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(33-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
2^{33}\equiv 5\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 9th term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(33-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

\[
2^{33}\equiv 5\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q802. The number \(392x6\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(42,36)=252
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 252.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(252\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(252\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q808. Between \(210\) and \(295\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 210 is 216.

\[
9\times 24=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 295.

\[
295=9\times 32+7
\]

\[
295-7=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{288-216}{9}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{295}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{209}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
32-23=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q813. Between \(104\) and \(242\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 104 is 108.

\[
18\times 6=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 234 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 242.

\[
242=18\times 13+8
\]

\[
242-8=234
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{234-108}{18}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{242}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{103}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
13-5=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[

\]

Final Answer

\[

\[

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(8\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+4=54
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+5=45
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-4|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-4|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q822. The number \(563x5\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=7
\]

\[
7-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
7-x=0
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
7-7=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\]

Fast result: \(728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(36-1)\bmod 10+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
7^{36}\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(36-1)\bmod 10+1=6
\]

\[
7^{36}\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q842. The number \(53x50\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=13
\]

\[
S+x=13+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q845. Between \(225\) and \(311\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 225 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 311.

\[
311=15\times 20+11
\]

\[
311-11=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-225}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{311}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{224}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-14=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q849. The number \(9x872\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 26; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
26+x=27
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
26+1=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q859. The number \(214x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 13.

\[
13+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q865. Between \(166\) and \(291\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 166 is 180.

\[
15\times 12=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 285 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 291.

\[
291=15\times 19+6
\]

\[
291-6=285
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{285-180}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{291}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{165}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-11=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q866. A number leaves the same remainder as \(7^{41}\) when divided by \(19\). What is that remainder?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(41-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 11.

\[
7^{41}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(41-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{41}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q872. Digits \(8\) and \(9\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+9=89
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+8=98
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-9|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-9|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q876. The number \(9x123\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q893. The number \(75x70\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=19
\]

\[
S+x=19+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q894. When \(7^{81}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(7\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(81-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
7^{81}\equiv 8\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 9th term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(81-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

\[
7^{81}\equiv 8\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q902. Between \(226\) and \(364\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(22\)
B. \(25\)
C. \(23\)
D. \(24\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 226 is 228.

\[
6\times 38=228
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 364.

\[
364=6\times 60+4
\]

\[
364-4=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{360-228}{6}+1=22+1=23
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 23.
Hence, there are 23 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(23\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{364}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{225}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
60-37=23
\]

Fast result: \(23\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q917. The number \(592x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification

\[
N=900
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(900\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(900\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q924. The number \(7x094\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q939. Between \(136\) and \(257\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(22\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 136 is 138.

\[
6\times 23=138
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 257.

\[
257=6\times 42+5
\]

\[
257-5=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{252-138}{6}+1=19+1=20
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 20.
Hence, there are 20 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(20\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{257}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{135}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
42-22=20
\]

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
282-12=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-105}{15}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{282}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{102}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-6=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

D. \(7\)

\[

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(37-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{37}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(37-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q968. The number \(170x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 10.

\[
10+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
10+x=15
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
10+5=15
\]
\[
15\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\]

\[

Q982. The number \(2x561\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=2
\]

\[
2-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
2-x=0
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
2-2=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=22
\]

\[
S+x=22+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q996. The number \(936x4\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 22; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q997. Bells ring at intervals of \(60\) and \(195\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(858\)
B. \(741\)
C. \(780\)
D. \(819\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
195=3\times 5\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,195)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,195)=\frac{60\times 195}{\operatorname{HCF}(60,195)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_divisibility_lcm_bridge

Q998. Power \(7^{39}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(39-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
7^{39}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(39-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
2^{39}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division

Q999. The power \(3^{97}\) is very large. What are its final three digits?
A. \(1059\)
B. \(915\)
C. \(963\)
D. \(1011\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{1000}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 24.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 27\rightarrow 81\rightarrow 243\rightarrow 729\rightarrow 187\rightarrow 561\rightarrow 683\rightarrow 49\rightarrow 147\rightarrow 441\rightarrow 323\rightarrow 969\rightarrow 907\rightarrow 721\rightarrow 163\rightarrow 489\rightarrow 467\rightarrow 401\rightarrow 203\rightarrow 609\rightarrow 827\rightarrow 481
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(97-1)\bmod 24+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{97}\equiv 3\pmod{1000}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(963\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(97-1)\bmod 24+1=1
\]

\[
3^{97}\equiv 3\pmod{1000}
\]

Fast result: \(963\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q1000. A number \(838\) is to be made divisible by \(24\). What is the minimum addition required?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
838=24\times 34+22
\]

\[
24-22=2
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 2.
\[
N=2
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_minimum_addition
